\## Key findings

Own repos — BytePort = orchestration, PhenoCompose = portable app-graph, phenotype-{registry,governance,audits,observability,specs} = policy/observability/GitOps spine. No single repo yet combines deploy orchestration AND a portable env abstraction — that interlock (BytePort×PhenoCompose) is the real gap, and phenotype-infra already stakes both + nanovms. Don't duplicate controllers.

Networking (your question) — your instinct to drop CF *quick* tunnels is right, but Tailscale Funnel ≠ implicit auth: it's *public* (gates who creates, not who visits), no custom domains, only 443/8443/10000, non-configurable throttle — not prod-grade. Correct topology: tailnet-serve for private (identity-gated, zero DNS) + Cloudflare *named* Tunnel + Access for public/custom domains (x.pheno.studio, projects.kooshapari.com). Funnel/quick-tunnel only for demos/webhooks.

Self-host PaaS (the swap layer) — all mature candidates (Coolify/Dokploy/CapRover) are Docker-first; podman works but is bleeding-edge. Coolify = primary (best API for BytePort to drive, git auto-deploy, multi-env, DB mgmt), Dokploy = fallback. Your podman-vs-docker choice cascades into this — that's the #1 decision to lock before architecture.

GitOps — Argo/Flux overkill for 1 node. Use GitHub native Environments as the approval gate (this is your "trio re-run then confirm"). Caveat: required-reviewers/wait-timers/deployment-branches on private repos is paid plan — confirm tier. Updates: Renovate primary + Dependabot Alerts on for vuln coverage.

IaC swap — Pulumi (Python) + Automation API recommended (python-native, per-stack secrets, one codebase → podman socket OR managed providers). Fallback: OpenTofu + render provider.

Migration hatch — default podman+quadlet; escape hatch WASI/Spin for uncarryable process workloads (→ NanoVM/Fly for syscall-heavy).

Two decisions needed before any architecture locks: (1) Docker Engine vs podman on WSL2; (2) GitHub plan tier. Ready to draft the formalized documents once you pick those.
